## Sage overview

<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Sage in the HMI library has 43 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, chromium, aluminium, tin, Cu, Fe and Zn from 4 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kowalska G · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland">The Safety Assessment of Toxic Metals in Commonly Used Herbs, Spices, Tea, and Coffee in Poland</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span href="../sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs">Determination of Heavy Metals Concentration in Traditional Herbs Commonly Consumed in the United Arab Emirates</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Environmental and Public Health</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in sage, as sold from 123 results in 3 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 29.4 to 1,290 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, GEMS/Food gave subgroup medians from 10 to 20 µg/kg from 97 results.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Kowalska 2021 reported lead at 2,210 µg/kg (mean) in sage, as sold from all samples.<sup class="cite">[[sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kowalska G · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland">The Safety Assessment of Toxic Metals in Commonly Used Herbs, Spices, Tea, and Coffee in Poland</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives 2022 reported cadmium at 40.8 µg/kg (mean) in sage, as sold from GEMS/Food contaminants database.<sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the sage came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

## What counts as sage

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>

href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-sage" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-sage-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of sage, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Sage, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>41–97 (bounded)</td><td>103–200 (bounded)</td><td>600</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>41–97 (bounded)</td><td>103–200 (bounded)</td><td>600</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>17 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>17</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for sage, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for sage, as sold on this basis in the completed sage ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach sage

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared sage from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The HMI reference for lead in sage, as sold pools 17 subgroup units from 1 sampling family, with a median of between 103 and 200 µg/kg and a middle half spanning between 41 and 97 (lower quartile) and 600 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For lead in sage, as sold, the reference median was 845 µg/kg, with a middle half from 490 to 935 µg/kg across 7 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium in sage, as sold, the reference median was between 11 and 15.6 µg/kg, with a middle half from below 9.5 to between 22 and 30.2 µg/kg across 12 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, sage, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (29.4 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (980 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in WPRO (17 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (20 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Targeted sage, as sold samples in EURO had lead lower at 68.2 µg/kg, against 620 µg/kg for EURO in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In another study, targeted sage, as sold samples in PAHO had lead higher at 1,290 µg/kg, against 849 µg/kg for PAHO in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>



<a id="processing-effects-and-derivatives"></a>
## Processing, cooking and preparation

<a id="hmi-ing-016"></a>
<a id="how-do-specified-processing-or-preparation-steps-change-the-measured-metal-endpoints"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-017"></a>
<a id="how-do-derivatives-concentrates-extracts-and-fractions-differ-from-their-parent-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-018"></a>
<a id="do-changes-reflect-removal-redistribution-moisture-or-yield-change-dilution-or-altered-species-or-availability"></a>

No processing study for metals in this food is in the library yet.



<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for sage

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing sage for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 1 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 200 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 4 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 100 µg/kg (Dghaim et al. 2015), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dghaim R, Al Khatib S, Rasool H, Khan MA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs">Determination of Heavy Metals Concentration in Traditional Herbs Commonly Consumed in the href="../sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for total arsenic ranged from 25 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 500 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from sage

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>

No source in the library converted sage concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Sage FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-sage" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-sage" data-question="What heavy metals are found in sage?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in sage?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, chromium, aluminium, tin, Cu, Fe and Zn in sage, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kowalska G · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland">The Safety Assessment of Toxic Metals in Commonly Used Herbs, Spices, Tea, and Coffee in Poland</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kowalska2021-metals-herbs-spices-tea-coffee-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dghaim R, Al Khatib S, Rasool H, Khan MA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs">Determination of Heavy Metals Concentration in Traditional Herbs Commonly Consumed in the United Arab Emirates</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Environmental and Public Health</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dghaim2015-heavy-metals-uae-herbs">Read source <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-sage" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-sage" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in sage?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in sage?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, sage, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (29.4 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (980 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in WPRO (17 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (20 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
